About AI discoveries in physics

This selection contains 180 catalog entries dated 6 Apr 2020 to 20 Nov 2026. 180 include structured evidence annotations describing reported tests, the AI contribution and remaining limits. Missing annotations mean those details have not been structured here, not that a study lacks evidence.

Dates follow the catalog publication policy. Coverage is selected and incomplete; entries can overlap or describe several results. Counts do not measure independent discoveries, clinical benefit or worldwide research output. Read the selection and evidence policy or compare catalog coverage by year and field.

20 Nov 2026University of Cambridge; Durham University; Stanford University

Machine-learned density functional theory reveals molecular control of electrolyte screening

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Machine-learned neural classical density functional theory trained on grand-canonical Monte Carlo data enabled calculations of asymptotic screening behavior across increasingly molecularly detailed electrolyte models. The authors report that solvent attraction creates a monotonic decay mode, solvent polarity produces re-entrant behavior, and ionic architecture changes screening-length exponents, challenging a single universal scaling law. These are computational predictions for modelled systems; the reviewed paper reports no experimental validation of the new predictions.

Technology: Neural classical density functional theory

Editorial relevance 53 physics PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Neural cDFT trained on grand-canonical Monte Carlo samples supplies direct correlation functions and enables efficient screening-pole analysis across electrolyte models.
Test and result
Computed decay lengths across primitive and molecular electrolyte models; neural cDFT approach compared against known primitive-model behavior.
Limitation / still unknown
No experimental validation of the new molecular-structure predictions is reported in the reviewed paper.

18 Nov 2026Department of Physics, University of Waterloo, Waterloo, Ontario, Canada

Geodesic-informed neural networks for testing spacetimes with S2

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Across five tested alternatives, the fit improvement over Schwarzschild did not offset the evidence penalty. The reported log Bayes factors span approximately -3.4 to -0.8 for the stated priors and dataset. Verified against an exact numerical solver and checked posterior approximation in appendices. The authors used existing observations without the newer GRAVITY astrometry; prior choices and data precision limit conclusions.

Technology: A trained geodesic-informed differentiable network represented each orbit to fit published S2 astrometry and line-of-sight velocities and obtain likelihood derivatives for model comparison.

Editorial relevance 53 physics Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A trained geodesic-informed differentiable network represented each orbit to fit published S2 astrometry and line-of-sight velocities and obtain likelihood derivatives for model comparison.
Test and result
Across five tested alternatives, the fit improvement over Schwarzschild did not offset the evidence penalty. The reported log Bayes factors span approximately -3.4 to -0.8 for the stated priors and dataset.
Limitation / still unknown
Verified against an exact numerical solver and checked posterior approximation in appendices. The authors used existing observations without the newer GRAVITY astrometry; prior choices and data precision limit conclusions.

17 Nov 2026CERN

Measured beam-loss reduction from shaped proton distributions at CERN accelerators

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: At CERN’s Super Proton Synchrotron, resonant excitation reshaped the vertical proton-beam distribution before extraction; the paper reports measured loss reductions up to 23.5%. Surrogate models based on generative deep learning supported rapid validation and Bayesian system identification, with separate measurements at the Proton Synchrotron. A larger reduction described in simulations is an extrapolation and is not presented here as measured performance; an unresolved short-spill surrogate divergence and lack of independent operational replication limit the claim.

Technology: A conditional generative deep-learning surrogate maps accelerator settings to beam distributions for rapid validation and system identification; beam-profile shaping itself was experimentally tested.

Editorial relevance 60 physics Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A conditional generative deep-learning surrogate maps accelerator settings to beam distributions for rapid validation and system identification; beam-profile shaping itself was experimentally tested.
Test and result
The primary v1 reports measured SPS beam profiles and loss-monitor outcomes, surrogate-to-measurement comparison, and separate Proton Synchrotron system-identification measurements.
Limitation / still unknown
The reported measured reductions are distinct from larger simulated extrapolations. Short-spill surrogate predictions diverged at larger settings for an unresolved reason; independent replication and sustained operational benefit were not shown.

16 Nov 2026Washington University in St. Louis

QORA designs quantum control sequences that extend spin coherence in diamond

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In an arXiv preprint, Washington University in St. Louis researchers report that QORA designed AID-ROD-1, a sequence that nearly doubled measured coherence time relative to DROID-C3PO in a dense ensemble of spin-1 nitrogen-vacancy centers in diamond. The agent system used simulation and laboratory feedback under human oversight. The research team subsequently analyzed the mechanisms behind the improvement. The comparison is specific to the tested ensemble, and independent replication was not established in the reviewed preprint.

Technology: QORA (GPT-6 Astra and Sorol)

Family GPT-6: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 60 physics Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
QORA proposed, simulated and refined control sequences using data from automated experiments under human oversight. The researchers interpreted the mechanism.
Test and result
Direct laboratory comparison of measured coherence time with DROID-C3PO in the tested spin-1 ensemble in diamond.
Limitation / still unknown
Independent replication and transfer to other platforms were not established in the reviewed preprint.
Evidence sources and annotation context

16 Nov 2026National Institute of Advanced Industrial Science and Technology; Nagaoka University of Technology

Breaking the Fixed-Substrate Limitation of Deep Learning Ellipsometry with Pseudo-Substrate Spectra

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding / tested result: FiLM-DLE takes both a thin-film spectrum and a pre-deposition pseudo-substrate spectrum as inputs. On 47 measured films across diverse substrates, its optical-constant and thickness estimates had mean absolute errors of 0.047, 0.030 and 1.52 nm, respectively, against manual analysis; each analysis took under two seconds.

Technology: Two-input deep-learning ellipsometry using pseudo-substrate spectra

Editorial relevance 60 physics PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
FiLM-DLE is a neural network that takes a thin-film spectrum and a measured pre-deposition substrate spectrum as inputs to estimate film optical constants and thickness.
Limitation / still unknown
The study evaluates the reported models and datasets only; broader generalization and real-world deployment outcomes were not established.

12 Nov 2026University of California, Berkeley; University of Cambridge

A neural quantum state variational Monte Carlo study of Aharonov-Casher bands

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Neural variational Monte Carlo wave functions, benchmarked against multiband exact diagonalization on small systems, were used to map phases in the Aharonov–Casher continuum model up to 36 electrons. At filling 2/3 the calculations identified an incommensurate electron Wigner crystal and a triangular hole-crystal phase; the study also estimated a uniform-field Jain-state freezing threshold. This is theoretical and model-specific, with no direct experimental confirmation. This concerns the Aharonov–Casher model and is distinct from the active artificial-graphene paired-Wigner-crystal result.

Technology: Neural-network-based variational Monte Carlo wave functions (a flux-attachment-informed ansatz and a Slater–Jastrow ansatz) represented competing correlated ground states, enabling multiband calculations beyond exact-diagonalization sizes.

Editorial relevance 53 physics Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Neural-network-based variational Monte Carlo wave functions (a flux-attachment-informed ansatz and a Slater–Jastrow ansatz) represented competing correlated ground states, enabling multiband calculations beyond exact-diagonalization sizes.
Test and result
Two neural variational wavefunctions were compared against multiband exact diagonalization on small systems; phase characterization used charge density, structure factors, pair correlations, Chern number and flux-pumping checks.
Limitation / still unknown
Predictions are specific to the continuum model and finite simulated systems; no direct experimental confirmation. Related active record 2026-04-study-10-21203-rs-3-rs-9316409-v1 concerns a distinct artificial-graphene paired crystal.
Evidence sources and annotation context

Evidence context (descriptions follow the cited sources; test type is a catalog classification):

Evidence sources: https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17144v1

9 Nov 2026University of Cambridge; University of Oxford

AI-Driven Discovery of Entanglement Witness in Spin Hamiltonians

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A Transformer-based entanglement classifier applied to spin-Hamiltonian states revealed a finite-temperature decision boundary that tracked the Jordan–Wigner excitation gap. Across XY, XXZ, frustrated J1–J2 and random-local Hamiltonians, the study reported strong classification against certified labels; results are numerical and theoretical, not experimental.

Technology: EntNet uses Transformer learning on 16 distilled local-unitary-invariant channels derived from two- to four-site Pauli correlators to classify many-body spin states as entangled or separable.

Editorial relevance 60 physics Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
EntNet uses Transformer learning on 16 distilled local-unitary-invariant channels derived from two- to four-site Pauli correlators to classify many-body spin states as entangled or separable.
Test and result
The study used synthetic states with separability labels and entanglement certificates, held out higher-order certificate classes, tested transfer across Hamiltonian families and checked 2,000 classically correlated separable states.
Limitation / still unknown
Numerical classification and model-based phase interpretation; no experimental system or separate-team replication was reported.

9 Nov 2026Department of Mechanical Engineering, University of Minnesota, Minneapolis, Minnesota, USA

Modeling and simulating turbulent secondary flow in a streamwise-rotating channel with LLM-guided symbolic regression and physics-informed neural networks

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: The authors report a newly derived closure for the secondary-flow covariance in streamwise-rotating channels. Compared candidate closures against published DNS statistics over five rotation conditions and evaluated the selected closures in the physics-informed simulation. Validation is computational against DNS; no independent physical experiment is reported.

Technology: LLM-guided symbolic regression generated candidate stress-closure equations; a physics-informed neural network then used the selected closure to simulate the flow.

7 Nov 2026Netaji Subhas University of Technology

Learned simulator analysis resolves interaction-dependent effects in a perovskite solar-cell model

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A LightGBM surrogate of a specified perovskite solar-cell simulator identified absorber-thickness and acceptor-doping effects that largely cancel when averaged across the sampled parameter space but reverse direction under different defect-density or band-alignment conditions. Original simulator data supported those conditional patterns. The finding concerns a computational response surface; no fabricated device or experimentally realized efficiency improvement was demonstrated.

Technology: LightGBM surrogate regression; Sobol global sensitivity analysis

Editorial relevance 33 physics Published article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The learned surrogate made a high-dimensional global sensitivity decomposition tractable and localized conditional parameter effects that pooled or single-parameter summaries concealed. Those interpretations were compared with original simulator data and explicit verification runs.
Test and result
A seven-parameter SCAPS-1D dataset retained 4,396 runs, with 3,525 training and 871 separately sampled test points. The selected LightGBM model was refitted on all retained runs for 262,144-evaluation sensitivity analysis. The source attributed 17.8% of modeled efficiency variance to interactions. Thickness and acceptor doping had near-zero first-order sensitivity but nonzero total effects; stratified original simulator data showed sign reversals. Separate simulator verification exposed wrong donor-density trends and overoptimistic ranking of the surrogate-selected optimum.
Limitation / still unknown
Results depend on the chosen simulator, layer stack, sampled ranges and numerical assumptions. Known defect-density effects were recovered rather than established as new physics. Surrogate sensitivity shares are not measured device variance, and original simulator checks are not external experimental replication. Several trends and selected-optimum ranking disagreed with direct simulation; no global physical optimum or fabricated high-efficiency device is claimed.
Evidence sources and annotation context

7 Nov 2026Anthropic

Claude agents prove a sharp spinless modular-bootstrap bound

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Claude agents derived a sharp asymptotic optimal-slope bound for the spinless modular bootstrap at large central charge. The proof artifact was checked in Lean and the result was also reviewed by a physicist. This is a formal theoretical result within the stated spinless setting, not an empirical or universal bound for other bootstrap settings.

Technology: Claude research agents with Lean formal verification

5 Nov 2026University of Münster; James Cook University; Utrecht University; Australian National University; Geological Survey of Denmark and Greenland

Deep-learning denoising reveals new short-period seismic observations of known ultra-low-velocity zones

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A supervised deep-learning denoising autoencoder exposed previously undocumented short-period S-diffracted wave observations associated with several known ultra-low-velocity zones (ULVZs). It also revealed four Hawaiian-ULVZ S-diff post-cursor records at Japan F-net stations from East Pacific Rise earthquakes, providing ray paths with mean azimuth about 78° from earlier North American paths. These new cross-cutting observations add constraints on ULVZ geometry and frequency/azimuth-dependent waveforms, but the paper does not present a new ULVZ detection or a completed structural inversion.

Technology: Supervised deep-learning denoising autoencoder (DeepSeisDAE)

Editorial relevance 40 physics Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The denoiser was trained on synthetic seismic waveforms mixed with real global noise, then used to separate signal from noise in real S-diff record sections. Its output helped researchers identify short-period post-cursor observations not reported in earlier literature, including four events recorded in Japan with nearly perpendicular ray geometry to previous North American observations of the Hawaiian ULVZ.
Test and result
A supervised deep-learning denoising autoencoder exposed previously undocumented short-period S-diffracted wave observations associated with several known ultra-low-velocity zones (ULVZs).
Limitation / still unknown
This paper reports new seismic observations sampling known ULVZs; it does not establish a new ULVZ structure. The Japanese post-cursors are difficult to separate from the main S-diff phases and require future 3D modelling to determine their interpretation and resolve trade-offs. Denoiser amplitude fidelity is a limitation, especially under training/application mismatch; the authors advise caution for single-station denoising. These are observational constraints, not a completed inversion of shape or composition.

2 Nov 2026Northwestern Polytechnical University; International Joint Institute of Intelligent Fluid Mechanics; National Key Laboratory of Aircraft Configuration Design

Symbolic regression derives aircraft aerodynamic scaling across sharp-cone and blunt-body geometries

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A Chain of Symbolic Regression (CoSR) analysis of hypersonic simulation data derived an interpretable non-power-product scaling relation for axial-force coefficient across sharp-cone and blunt-body configurations. It combines angle of attack, Mach number and Reynolds number through a dominant term and a viscous correction; the relation has a shared functional form but geometry-specific fitted constants. The authors interpret the constants as reflecting differences in shock/base drag, friction drag and pressure drag. This is a new model-derived scaling result from simulations, not a wind-tunnel or flight measurement.

Technology: Chain of Symbolic Regression (CoSR), a progressive symbolic-regression framework for dimensionless physical relations

Editorial relevance 47 physics Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
CoSR progressively searched for interpretable dimensionless groups and combined their relationships instead of fitting one end-to-end expression. Applied to hypersonic simulation data, it identified a dominant angle-of-attack/Mach term and a Reynolds-number-dependent viscous correction, then fitted geometry-specific constants for two body shapes. Earlier canonical-law and fluid examples in the same paper are framework validations and are not counted separately here.
Test and result
A Chain of Symbolic Regression (CoSR) analysis of hypersonic simulation data derived an interpretable non-power-product scaling relation for axial-force coefficient across sharp-cone and blunt-body configurations.
Limitation / still unknown
The aerodynamic relation was inferred from hypersonic simulation data for two configurations (sharp cone and blunt body), not independently measured flight or wind-tunnel data. It is a parameterized model over the analyzed configuration/data range; external validation and broader geometry coverage are not established in the source. The simpler dominant component is linked to known hypersonic invariance, while the learned viscous correction and geometry-specific scaling provide the paper’s new modeled relationship. Do not treat the authors’ drag-component interpretations as direct measurements.

30 Oct 2026LHCb Collaboration; CERN

LHCb reports first single-experiment evidence for B⁰ → μ⁺μ⁻ decay

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Using 18 fb⁻¹ of Run 3 data and earlier LHCb data, the collaboration reports first single-experiment evidence for the rare B⁰→μ⁺μ⁻ decay, with a combined significance of 3.2σ and measured branching fraction (1.14 +0.42/−0.39 stat. +0.06/−0.05 syst.)×10⁻¹⁰. A boosted decision tree classified candidates and suppressed combinatorial background in the fit. This is evidence below the 5σ discovery threshold; results agree with Standard Model expectations. The date is the original preprint submission; CERN announced the result on 3 November 2026.

Technology: LHCb boosted decision tree (BDT) candidate classifier and multivariate trigger classifiers

Editorial relevance 53 physics Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The primary analysis uses a BDT to suppress combinatorial background, scores candidates and divides the dimuon mass fit into five response bins to improve signal sensitivity; it also uses multivariate classifiers in the software trigger. CERN states that machine-learning background suppression, together with detector upgrades, enabled the analysis to isolate these rare signals. The measured evidence comes from the collaboration's collision data and statistical fit, not an AI prediction.
Test and result
Using 18 fb⁻¹ of Run 3 data and earlier LHCb data, the collaboration reports first single-experiment evidence for the rare B⁰→μ⁺μ⁻ decay, with a combined significance of 3.2σ and measured branching fraction (1.14 +0.42/−0.39 stat.
Limitation / still unknown
This is a preprint submitted to Physical Review X and reports 3.2σ evidence, not a 5σ observation. Earlier joint LHCb–CMS evidence was not confirmed by later Run 1–2 analyses; this result is the first single-experiment evidence, not the first ever claim. The paper reports all measurements as consistent with Standard Model expectations. Machine learning contributes to candidate selection/background control along with substantial detector upgrades.

Technology: Random forest feature importance; extra-trees regression

Editorial relevance 40 physics Published article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution

Test and result

RF nonlinear feature importance distinguished features associated with fundamental resonance frequency from those associated with quality factor. Twelve regression families were compared using random within-design splits, with a 70:30 split used for later interpolation analyses.
Limitation / still unknown
Surface roughness was assigned per coating material and strongly correlated with surface category; several physical descriptors were nominal rather than device-specific measurements. Random splitting within sampled wafers tests interpolation, not unseen-material or independent-wafer generalization. Feature-importance rankings do not establish causal mechanisms, independently optimized devices or universal material laws.

27 Oct 2026Not stated in the reviewed material

Planar energetics do not determine twin persistence in nanocrystalline aluminium

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Two learned potentials that reproduce similar planar slip energetics predicted markedly different retained twin coverage (64% versus 14%) from the same precursor under identical loading. The analysis identifies moving-core forces, local traction orientation, and asynchronous partial-slip fronts as determinants that planar energetic descriptors miss. The paper benchmarks Al-QP against five existing models on published DFT data and uses paired first-principles defect calculations to check local forces and energy margins. The result is computational; no experimental confirmation is claimed.

Technology: A MACE-based machine-learned interatomic potential (Al-QP), trained on strain-coupled fault, twin, and interface configurations, enabled atomistic simulations of twin formation and persistence in nanocrystalline aluminium.

The comparison concerns one fixed setup and subsets of a recorded acquisition. Test prediction error was about4.38dB; noise, model mismatch and temporal variation were not separately isolated. More flexible PGNN had lower prediction error at larger budgets. Classical phase-coherence observations and perfect-CSI simulation bounds are separate from the learned result.

20 Oct 2026Georgia Institute of Technology

Learned bubble reconstruction shows focus-dependent distribution agreement

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A convolutional multitask model trained on simulated optical images was applied without experimental fine-tuning to bubble shadowgraphs at four radial planes. Reconstructed size distributions overlapped in-focus experimental references by approximately 68–92%, with poorer agreement farther from the focal reference. This is a configuration-specific population measurement validation; experimental three-dimensional coordinates were not independently known for each bubble.

Technology: Focus-conditioned convolutional multitask neural network

Editorial relevance 33 physics PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A learned convolutional appearance representation, conditioned on focal-plane position, jointly infers size and depth from optical signatures and enables the scoped distribution-level reference comparison.
Test and result
The model jointly predicts depth relative to the known focal plane and physical-equivalent diameter. Experimental populations contained 142, 971, 859 and 204 reconstructed bubbles; distributions were compared with plane-specific in-focus references. A separate 11,679-bubble center-plane reference comparison is reported.
Limitation / still unknown
The 97.6% normalized depth accuracy belongs to synthetic data, not experimental depth truth. Model training describes a randomized crop development split alongside a timestamp-separated test; these partitions should not be conflated. Experimental references are distribution-level and share the imaging facility. Center-reference comparisons use differing summaries (approximately 92% versus 86% overlap); no single pooled value is inferred. Optical configuration, segmentation, overlap and defocus limit transfer; no general three-dimensional accuracy or improved flow process was established.

16 Oct 2026Norwegian University of Science and Technology

Two-stage learned model forecasts hydrogen dispersion from early sensor observations

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: An MLP ensemble estimated leak rate from early sensor readings, then a sparse Gaussian-process model predicted hydrogen concentration in a confined-channel dataset. The dataset combined 22 physical release tests and 10 CFD cases, representing 26 unique scenarios. Reported leak-rate MAE was 0.052 g/s; the selected dispersion model had concentration-volume-fraction MAE 0.0176 and RMSE 0.0226 on four test experiments. Candidate models were compared on that test set before final selection. This retrospective proof of concept does not establish ship-compartment transfer, operational safety benefit or prospective performance.

8 Oct 2026ATLAS Collaboration; CERN

ATLAS graph neural network enables first LHC observation of single-top s-channel production

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Using 140 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton–proton collisions, ATLAS reports the first observation of single-top-quark s-channel production at the LHC, with 5.1σ observed (5.2σ expected) significance. The measured cross-section is 10.8 ± 1.5(stat) ± 1.6(syst) pb, consistent with the Standard Model. A graph neural network discriminant separates the signal from top-pair, W+jets and t-channel single-top backgrounds; the analysis also uses broadened event acceptance, improved b tagging and refined modeling.

Technology: Event-level graph neural network; ATLAS GN2 graph-transformer b-jet tagger

Editorial relevance 60 physics Press releasePreprint Suggest a correction

The GNN uses event kinematics to distinguish s-channel signal from major backgrounds, with its score distribution used in the likelihood fit. Test and result
Using 140 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton–proton collisions, ATLAS reports the first observation of single-top-quark s-channel production at the LHC, with 5.1σ observed (5.2σ expected) significance.
Limitation / still unknown
This is an ATLAS preprint submitted to PRL as of the checked date; prior ATLAS analyses had reported evidence, and the Tevatron had already observed the process in a combined CDF+DØ result. The cross-section has about 20% total uncertainty and agrees with the Standard Model; the network is one of several improvements contributing to sensitivity, not the sole cause.

GPT-6 Astra assists a gauge-invariant lattice chiral-fermion measure construction

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Nathaniel Craig proposes a smooth, exponentially local, gauge-invariant fermion measure on admissible four-dimensional lattices, with restrictions covering the Standard Model gauge groups. GPT-6 Astra helped develop the refinement construction and led the detailed proof. The supplementary report states that Lean kernel replay accepted the encoded measure-existence result and supporting inputs under their recorded assumptions. This is a candidate finite-lattice definition; reflection positivity, practical simulation and an interacting continuum theory remain outside the established claim. The October 6, 2026 preprint preceded the November 15 institutional announcement.

5 Oct 2026The University of Tokyo; Los Alamos National Laboratory

Recurrent neural network reveals near-critical seeding of topological defects in Kibble–Zurek simulations

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In numerical simulations of a one-dimensional Landau–Ginzburg phase transition, a recurrent neural network predicted the final locations of topological defects from short sequences of order-parameter fluctuations during the Kibble–Zurek impulse regime, before freeze-out and before the defects were macroscopically visible. Prediction error across quench rates followed power-law scaling consistent with the Kibble–Zurek freeze-out time, indicating that information about the eventual defect pattern is encoded in near-critical fluctuations in this model.

Technology: Recurrent neural network (RNN); LSTM and Transformer architectures were also checked

Editorial relevance 47 physics Published articlePreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The RNN analyzed short time sequences from numerical order-parameter dynamics and inferred the final kink count and positions from small fluctuations during the impulse regime. This allowed the authors to identify when information about future defect positions becomes predictable, a quantity not directly provided by conventional Kibble–Zurek defect-density scaling. They report similar prediction behavior with LSTM and Transformer models, suggesting the result is not specific to one architecture.
Test and result
In numerical simulations of a one-dimensional Landau–Ginzburg phase transition, a recurrent neural network predicted the final locations of topological defects from short sequences of order-parameter fluctuations during the Kibble–Zurek impulse regime, before freeze-out and before the defects were macroscopically visible.
Limitation / still unknown
This is a computational result from a noisy one-dimensional Landau–Ginzburg model, not an experimental observation of defects in a material or cosmological system. The paper uses overdamped dynamics (η=1), low noise temperature θ=10⁻⁸, a finite periodic grid and simulated training/test samples; similar behavior at another noise setting is reported in supplementary material. The finding is consistent with the Kibble–Zurek mechanism and an earlier deconstruction of its dynamics; it does not establish that this prediction method applies unchanged to all phase transitions. The PRL article was published 2026-10-05; the original arXiv preprint was submitted 2025-08-28 and revised as v2 on 2026-09-10.

18 Sep 2026LHCb Collaboration; CERN

LHCb observes the doubly charmed Ωcc⁺ baryon with an 8.7σ global significance

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Using 6.3 fb⁻¹ of 13.6 TeV proton-proton collisions recorded in 2024, LHCb observed an Ωc⁰π⁺ mass peak with 8.7σ global significance and identified it as the weakly decaying doubly charmed Ωcc⁺ baryon. The reported mass is 3725.9 ± 1.0 (stat) ± 0.2 (syst) ± 0.4 (lifetime) ± 0.6 (external) MeV/c². A boosted decision tree suppressed random-track combinatorial background; the result is a preprint submitted to Physical Review Letters.

Technology: Boosted decision tree classifier (TMVA toolkit)

Editorial relevance 60 physics Preprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The BDT was trained on simulated Ωcc⁺ signal decays and wrong-charge Ωc⁰π⁻ combinations from data and used to suppress the dominant random-track combinatorial background in the offline candidate selection. The background rejection supported the mass-spectrum analysis yielding the first observation.
Test and result
Using 6.3 fb⁻¹ of 13.6 TeV proton-proton collisions recorded in 2024, LHCb observed an Ωc⁰π⁺ mass peak with 8.7σ global significance and identified it as the weakly decaying doubly charmed Ωcc⁺ baryon.
Limitation / still unknown
The source is an LHCb collaboration preprint submitted to PRL, not yet a peer-reviewed journal publication as of its arXiv version. The observed peak has 8.7σ global significance; the mass uncertainty includes an explicit lifetime-dependent selection-bias term because the Ωcc⁺ lifetime is unknown. BDT use is an analysis component, not the sole basis of the observation.

31 Aug 2026Brookhaven National Laboratory

GPT-5.6 helps prove an exact mapping between frustrated and field-driven spin chains

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A single-author preprint proves that every finite one-dimensional open O(n) spin chain with arbitrary inhomogeneous nearest- and next-nearest-neighbor interactions has an exact, temperature-independent Hamiltonian mapping to a simpler nearest-neighbor chain with axial single-spin potentials. A parallel result for q-state Potts chains yields a closed-form solution for every q ≥ 2 and finite chain length. The theorem generalizes a 1990 Heisenberg-chain challenge and connects spontaneous collective behavior from competing interactions to field-induced behavior.

Technology: OpenAI GPT-5.6 Sol; GPT-5.6 Sol Pro for final adversarial manuscript review

Family GPT-5: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 60 physics PreprintPreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The author credits GPT-5.6 Sol with scientific discovery, including generating the recursive Householder moving-frame proof and recognizing broad generalizations. The author identified the hidden reciprocity, extended the theorem to arbitrary spatial inhomogeneity, and states that all AI-generated scientific content was reviewed and verified. GPT-5.6 Sol Pro conducted a final adversarial review.
Test and result
A single-author preprint proves that every finite one-dimensional open O(n) spin chain with arbitrary inhomogeneous nearest- and next-nearest-neighbor interactions has an exact, temperature-independent Hamiltonian mapping to a simpler nearest-neighbor chain with axial single-spin potentials.
Limitation / still unknown
This is a single-author arXiv preprint, not yet peer reviewed. The theorem concerns classical one-dimensional open chains and exact Hamiltonian mappings; the paper discusses possible experimental relevance but reports no experimental test. The author assumes responsibility for reviewing and verifying AI-generated content; Mathematica 14.3 was also used to verify results.

4 Aug 2026ATLAS Collaboration; CERN

Boosted decision tree enables first observation of two simultaneously longitudinally polarized Z bosons

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Using 164 fb⁻¹ of 13.6 TeV proton–proton data and a statistical combination with the Run 2 measurement, ATLAS reports the first observation of ZZ production with both Z bosons longitudinally polarized, at 6.5σ observed (5.6σ expected). The combined signal strength is 1.22 ± 0.21; the Run 3 measurement also reports polarization fractions across the fiducial phase space and two four-lepton invariant-mass ranges, consistent with Standard Model predictions.

Technology: Boosted decision tree (BDT) classifier

Editorial relevance 60 physics Press releasePreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Preprint · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The BDT is trained on polarization-sensitive lepton angular observables to separate longitudinal-longitudinal ZZ events from transverse or mixed-polarization events. Its score distribution is the fitted observable used to extract the signal strength and significance.
Test and result
Using 164 fb⁻¹ of 13.6 TeV proton–proton data and a statistical combination with the Run 2 measurement, ATLAS reports the first observation of ZZ production with both Z bosons longitudinally polarized, at 6.5σ observed (5.6σ expected).
Limitation / still unknown
The ATLAS paper was submitted to PRL as a preprint on 2026-07-14; the cited official ATLAS briefing is dated 2026-08-04. The 6.5σ first observation combines the Run 3 analysis with the prior Run 2 measurement, whose standalone significance was 4.3σ. The Run 3 analysis alone reports 5.1σ. The measured fractions agree with the Standard Model; the statistical uncertainty dominates and polarization-template modeling is the leading systematic uncertainty. This is an experimental observation from ATLAS data, not evidence of physics beyond the Standard Model.

15 May 2026Université Grenoble Alpes; Institut de Physique du Globe de Paris; University of Tokyo; Service des dangers naturels, Valais

Unsupervised learning separates rockfall and glacier-sliding precursors before the Blatten collapse

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Unsupervised clustering of 20 days of seismic data before the 28 May 2025 Blatten glacier collapse separated rockfall signals from events associated with basal glacier sliding. Sliding-related event counts accelerated approximately exponentially beginning about five days before collapse, with further acceleration about 1.5 days beforehand; the authors interpret this pattern as consistent with nucleation-like slip weakening.

Technology: Deep scattering network, independent component analysis and hierarchical clustering (unsupervised machine learning)

Editorial relevance 47 physics Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The unsupervised representation and clustering explored continuous single-station seismic records without event templates, separating rockfall, glacier-sliding and cultural-noise signatures. This made the evolving sliding-related event population and its acceleration measurable in the 20-day record.
Test and result
Unsupervised clustering of 20 days of seismic data before the 28 May 2025 Blatten glacier collapse separated rockfall signals from events associated with basal glacier sliding.
Limitation / still unknown
The finding is a retrospective analysis of one event, relying primarily on a single seismic station 5.3 km from the site. The authors describe the physical mechanism as consistent with nucleation and hypothesize that rockfalls contributed to instability; these interpretations require study of other events. The inferred slip-rate proxy has high absolute uncertainty, and the approach is not an operational early-warning system.

12 May 2026Southern University of Science and Technology; Institute of Geomechanics, Chinese Academy of Geological Sciences; ETH Zürich

Machine-learning earthquake catalog reveals scale-dependent fault controls at Gonghe geothermal site

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A machine-learning workflow catalogued 7,346 microearthquakes (ML −2.0 to 2.18) recorded during 2021 stimulation and circulation tests at China’s Gonghe enhanced-geothermal-system site. The catalog resolves a ring-shaped distribution with four clusters and varying faulting styles. The authors infer that site-scale seismicity reactivated mapped, pre-existing natural faults; at borehole scale, the principal seismogenic zone coincides with strong SHmax rotation. Stress modeling estimates a low fault-friction coefficient near 0.23 (95% CI 0.19–0.48) and the low-to-moderate fluid overpressure needed for slip.

Technology: Machine-learning earthquake-detection and cataloging workflow (model details are not stated in the publisher abstract)

Test and result

20 Mar 2026CMS Collaboration

ParticleNet enables first hadron-collider W-boson mass measurement from hadronic decays

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Using 138 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton–proton data, CMS reports the first measurement of the W-boson mass in an all-jets final state at a hadron collider: 80.83 ± 0.55 GeV. The result comes from unfolded groomed jet-mass distributions for boosted hadronic W+jets events. The mass-decorrelated ParticleNet graph neural network tagger provides the main result by selecting the W-jet sample and suppressing quark/gluon multijet background; a non-neural N₂ tagger is used as a cross-check.

Technology: ParticleNet mass-decorrelated graph neural network jet tagger

Editorial relevance 47 physics Published articlePreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The analysis uses ParticleNet as its main W-jet tagger. Its graph neural network treats jet constituents as an unordered particle cloud and classifies W→qq′ jets against QCD multijet backgrounds; its mass-decorrelated output defines the signal/control regions used for the primary measurement. The N₂ substructure observable is a separate cross-check.
Test and result
Using 138 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton–proton data, CMS reports the first measurement of the W-boson mass in an all-jets final state at a hadron collider: 80.83 ± 0.55 GeV.
Limitation / still unknown
This is an all-jets boosted-W extraction with 0.55 GeV total uncertainty, much less precise than the CMS leptonic-channel W-mass result; the paper frames it as a first step toward future all-jets precision measurements and as a test of jet-substructure modeling. The earlier preliminary CMS result of 80.77 ± 0.57 GeV was superseded by this same paper’s published 80.83 ± 0.55 GeV result and is not counted separately. Jet tagging, QCD background estimation, unfolding, hadronization and jet-energy modeling contribute analysis dependence and uncertainties.

5 Mar 2026University of Birmingham; University of Cambridge

Time-delay neural networks expose three fault-development phases across laboratory pressure regimes

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A time-delay neural network trained on multi-parameter acoustic-emission records from Alzo-granite triaxial tests at 5–40 MPa resolved three recurring phases: microcrack nucleation marked by waveform-scattering changes, fault growth with evolving spatial and magnitude distributions, and coalescence marked by peak-delay increases and b-value changes. The model also predicted stress-drop timing and size across axial-splitting and shear-localization failures. This is a laboratory result, not a natural-earthquake precursor claim.

Technology: Genetic-algorithm-optimized time-delay neural network (TDNN)

Editorial relevance 53 physics Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution

Leave-one-experiment-out validation tested cross-condition generalization.
Test and result
A time-delay neural network trained on multi-parameter acoustic-emission records from Alzo-granite triaxial tests at 5–40 MPa resolved three recurring phases: microcrack nucleation marked by waveform-scattering changes, fault growth with evolving spatial and magnitude distributions, and coalescence marked by peak-delay increases and b-value changes.
Limitation / still unknown
The analysis uses four laboratory experiments in Alzo granite, so results are sample- and apparatus-limited and do not establish natural-earthquake predictability. 25 Feb 2026CMS Collaboration; CERN Large Hadron Collider

First observation of single-top-quark production with a W and Z boson at CMS

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: CMS reports the first observation of single-top-quark production in association with a W and Z boson in proton-proton collisions. A Particle Transformer classifier and improved reconstruction separated the rare tWZ signal from major backgrounds in three- and four-lepton events. Combining 13 and 13.6 TeV data (200 fb⁻¹), the measured signal has 5.8σ observed significance (3.5σ expected) and cross sections of 248 ± 52 fb at 13 TeV and 242 ± 77 fb at 13.6 TeV. The result agrees with the Standard Model within 2.3σ.

Technology: Particle Transformer (ParT) multiclass classifier

Editorial relevance 60 physics Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
CMS trained four ParT models, split by three- versus four-lepton signal region and 13 versus 13.6 TeV samples. In the three-lepton region, a four-node classifier distinguished tWZ, ttZ and other backgrounds; in the four-lepton region, a binary classifier separated tWZ from backgrounds. Its classifier-score categories entered the statistical signal extraction. The paper reports a substantial sensitivity gain versus the previous analysis: expected significance on the reused 13 TeV data rose from 1.4σ to 3.0σ, contributing to the first observation when combined with 13.6 TeV data.
Test and result
CMS reports the first observation of single-top-quark production in association with a W and Z boson in proton-proton collisions.
Limitation / still unknown
This is the first observation of the Standard-Model tWZ production process, not evidence for physics beyond the Standard Model. A 2024 CMS analysis had reported earlier evidence at 3.4σ; the 2026 paper adds 13.6 TeV Run 3 data and improved reconstruction/ML analysis to reach observation. The 5.8σ result combines 13 and 13.6 TeV datasets. Cross sections and signal strength are consistent with the Standard Model within the reported uncertainties.

24 Feb 2026Pennsylvania State University; Georgia Institute of Technology

Deep-learning tracking of mantle simulations finds continental-margin subduction zones persist longer

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A fully convolutional network segmented subduction zones in two-dimensional mantle-convection simulations using temperature, vertical velocity and fineness fields. Across the modeled runs, zones forming near continental margins were generally longer-lived and less sensitive to internal heating than oceanic zones, supporting the hypothesis that continental lithosphere can stabilize subduction during early-Earth-like conditions. This is a computational-model result rather than direct observation of ancient Earth.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A random-forest regression model trained on seismic waveform features predicted GNSS-derived displacement-rate changes over roughly six years in western Shikoku, Japan. Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A Random Forest using a network representation of acoustic-emission event catalogs predicted the timing of meter-scale laboratory mainshocks from tens of seconds to milliseconds ahead (test-set log R²=0.84). This is a laboratory-scale result, not a validated method for forecasting natural earthquakes.

11 Sep 2025Brookhaven National Laboratory

AI-assisted transfer-matrix analysis yields an exact solution for the 1D frustrated q-state Potts model

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: The study exactly solves the one-dimensional J₁–J₂ q-state Potts model for arbitrary q by reducing its q²×q² transfer matrix to a 2×2 maximally symmetric subspace and mapping it to an effective q-state Potts model in a field. OpenAI’s o3-mini-high reasoning model helped derive the q=3 case; the author then generalized the result to arbitrary q and checked the formulas numerically. The solution nontrivially extends an older mapping known for q=2/Ising and yields q-dependent phase and residual-entropy behavior.

14 Aug 2025King Abdullah University of Science and Technology (KAUST); GEOMAR Helmholtz Centre for Ocean Research

Machine-learning earthquake catalog reveals transform fault in the Zabargad Fracture Zone

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A 12-month network of ocean-bottom and land seismometers yielded a high-resolution catalog of more than 3,900 earthquakes in the northern Red Sea Zabargad Fracture Zone and Mabahiss Deep. The southern cluster’s diffuse seismicity and predominantly left-lateral strike-slip focal mechanisms reveal a NE–SW transform fault, which the authors describe as possibly in an early development stage. The northern cluster instead shows NW–SE normal-faulting lineaments related to extension.

Technology: OBSTransformer deep-learning phase picker, transfer-learned from EQTransformer and used through SeisBench

Editorial relevance 53 physics Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The researchers compared EQTransformer, OBSTransformer and PickBlue for automatic earthquake detection and P/S phase picking on continuous ocean-bottom and land-station data. OBSTransformer was selected because it detected more events than EQTransformer while yielding fewer false detections than PickBlue and closer agreement with manual picks. The resulting detections and picks were manually checked and corrected, then used in the catalog from which the clusters and focal-mechanism analysis revealed the faulting pattern.
Test and result
A 12-month network of ocean-bottom and land seismometers yielded a high-resolution catalog of more than 3,900 earthquakes in the northern Red Sea Zabargad Fracture Zone and Mabahiss Deep.
Limitation / still unknown
The transform fault is inferred from the southern seismicity pattern and selected-event focal mechanisms, not directly imaged along its full geometry; the authors describe it as possibly in an early development stage. The catalog covers a 12-month deployment and is a snapshot that may not capture episodic ridge seismicity. Exact spreading-axis configuration remains uncertain. The earthquake-processing workflow was not fully automatic: investigators manually reviewed and corrected picks, removed events recorded by fewer than three stations, and used manual focal-mechanism analysis.

10 Aug 2025Northwestern Polytechnical University; Pennsylvania State University

Symbolic-regression turbulence analysis separates transferable flow physics from dataset-specific closure terms

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Using high-fidelity data for plane channels, zero-pressure-gradient turbulent boundary layers and adverse-pressure-gradient boundary layers, the authors used symbolic regression to decompose learned turbulence-closure corrections into inner-layer, outer-layer and pressure-gradient components. They report that retaining the generalisable inner-layer term and adding flow-specific corrections improved predictions on held-out boundary-layer and aerofoil configurations, including unseen geometries and Reynolds numbers, outperforming baseline RANS closures in a priori and a posteriori tests. The authors identify conflation of transferable and training-specific flow behavior as a source of poor model generalization in the tested setting.

Technology: Symbolic regression applied to high-fidelity turbulence simulation data

Editorial relevance 47 physics Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Symbolic regression generated interpretable closure expressions from progressively expanded flow datasets. Decomposition of the learned terms let the researchers distinguish inner-layer components that transferred across flow configurations from outer-layer and pressure-gradient corrections that were specific to flow classes, enabling improved out-of-training-flow predictions.
Test and result
Using high-fidelity data for plane channels, zero-pressure-gradient turbulent boundary layers and adverse-pressure-gradient boundary layers, the authors used symbolic regression to decompose learned turbulence-closure corrections into inner-layer, outer-layer and pressure-gradient components.
Limitation / still unknown
The finding concerns model-derived closure components and validation on selected numerical flow configurations, not a new direct physical measurement. The claimed root cause of poor generalization is the authors' conclusion about data-driven turbulence closures in the studied context, not a universal causal theorem about all turbulence models. Out-of-sample tests and comparative RANS simulations support the stated transfer result.

29 Jul 2025LHCb Collaboration; CERN

LHCb makes the first high-significance observation of the rare Σ⁺ → pμ⁺μ⁻ decay

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Using 5.4 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton-proton collision data collected in 2016–2018, LHCb observed 237 ± 16 Σ⁺ → pμ⁺μ⁻ decays and measured a branching fraction of (1.08 ± 0.17) × 10⁻⁸. The dimuon spectrum showed no evidence of resonant structures, and the results are compatible with Standard Model expectations. This is the first high-significance observation; earlier HyperCP and LHCb Run 1 results were evidence only.

Technology: LHCb boosted decision tree (BDT) classifier

Editorial relevance 60 physics Published articlePreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The BDT combined kinematic and geometric variables, trained on simulated signal and data sidebands representing combinatorial background, and was used in the final candidate selection. Its output threshold was optimized together with particle-identification and Λ-veto criteria to maximize expected significance. This classifier contributed to background suppression; it was one component of the analysis, alongside upgraded trigger selections, detector reconstruction, and the statistical fit.
Test and result
Using 5.4 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton-proton collision data collected in 2016–2018, LHCb observed 237 ± 16 Σ⁺ → pμ⁺μ⁻ decays and measured a branching fraction of (1.08 ± 0.17) × 10⁻⁸.
Limitation / still unknown
The paper reports an observation of the nonresonant rare decay, not evidence for the hypothetical intermediate X⁰ suggested by the earlier HyperCP candidates; no resonant dimuon structure is found. The measured branching fraction is compatible with Standard Model expectations. AI supported event selection and background reduction but is not the sole basis of the result.

16 Jul 2025LHCb Collaboration; CERN

LHCb observes CP violation for the first time in baryon decays

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Using 9 fb⁻¹ of LHCb Run 1 and Run 2 data, the collaboration measured a phase-space-averaged CP asymmetry of (2.45 ± 0.46 ± 0.10)% in Λb⁰ → pK⁻π⁺π⁻ and its conjugate, a 5.2σ observation. In the resonance-dominated R(pπ⁺π⁻)K⁻ region, the asymmetry was (5.4 ± 0.9 ± 0.1)%, 6.0σ from zero. Machine-learning BDT selection suppressed combinatorial background; neural-network PID variables mitigated particle misidentification.

Technology: Boosted decision tree event classifier; neural-network particle-identification variables

Editorial relevance 60 physics Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The BDT used decay topology, displacement and kinematics to suppress random combinations in the four-track signal selection. Neural-network PID variables helped mitigate misidentified final-state particle backgrounds. These tools supported signal purity for the baryon–antibaryon asymmetry measurement.
Test and result
Using 9 fb⁻¹ of LHCb Run 1 and Run 2 data, the collaboration measured a phase-space-averaged CP asymmetry of (2.45 ± 0.46 ± 0.10)% in Λb⁰ → pK⁻π⁺π⁻ and its conjugate, a 5.2σ observation.
Limitation / still unknown
This is the first reported baryon-decay CP-violation observation, with significance 5.2σ for the phase-space-integrated result and 6.0σ in the specified resonance region. Reported asymmetries average over phase space; the paper says a full amplitude-structure investigation remains future work. Small asymmetries may cancel across angular-momentum amplitudes. The measured CP asymmetry is distinct from raw yield asymmetry; production/detection nuisance asymmetries are subtracted using a control channel.

30 Apr 2025Stanford University

Regional deep-learning phase picking reveals 24 additional earthquakes in a Gorda Ridge swarm

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A long-window regional phase picker and phase-association workflow detected 34 earthquakes in a 29 February 2024 swarm near the Gorda Ridge, offshore northern California: all 10 reported by the USGS plus 24 additional events. The detections were possible from stations roughly 300 km from the swarm, beyond the distance range represented by standard local-event pickers. This is one paper-level catalog result, not 24 separate discoveries.

28 Mar 2025Academia Sinica; National Central University; National Taiwan University; Central Weather Administration of Taiwan

Deep-learning seismic catalog reveals a potentially reactivated blind fault in Taiwan’s 2025 Dapu earthquake sequence

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A RED-PAN deep-learning phase picker enabled a catalog of 6,805 relocated events for the 11-day sequence following the M6.4 Dapu earthquake. Analysis of those events, focal mechanisms and velocity structure identified an east-dipping plane at about 5–10 km and a west-dipping plane at about 10–15 km; the authors infer the mainshock involved a previously unrecognized blind west-dipping fault and interactions with the known Muchiliao–Liuchia system. This is a localized structural interpretation, and the authors describe some fault associations as potential.

10 Feb 2025Massachusetts Institute of Technology

OptPDE identifies new candidate integrable PDE families with nontrivial conserved quantities

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Automatic differentiation optimized partial-differential-equation coefficients to increase the number of conserved quantities. The published study reports four PDE families with at least one nontrivial conserved quantity and analytically studies one new candidate, u_t = u_x^3; the work establishes candidate systems and conserved quantities, not that every generated family is fully integrable.

Technology: OptPDE coefficient optimization with the CQFinder conserved-quantity search

Editorial relevance 47 physics Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The authors explicitly describe OptPDE as a machine-learning approach. CQFinder computes the conserved-quantity objective from a symbolic PDE basis; OptPDE uses a sigmoid-smoothed objective and backpropagation to optimize 34 trainable PDE coefficients. They ran 5,000 random initializations and used dimensionality reduction to group the outputs into four families. This is a learned, trainable symbolic search, not simply use of an automatic-differentiation library.
Test and result
Automatic differentiation optimized partial-differential-equation coefficients to increase the number of conserved quantities.
Limitation / still unknown
The peer-reviewed source reports four PDE families with at least one nontrivial conserved quantity and analytically examines one new candidate, u_t = u_x^3. The authors explicitly state that maximizing conserved quantities does not guarantee integrability; the result is a concrete candidate system and its studied properties, not proof that every output family is integrable.

6 Feb 2025China Earthquake Administration; Institute of Geophysics; Institute of Earthquake Forecasting

AI-built Baihetan reservoir catalog reveals fault-controlled and depth-dependent seismicity

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: LOC-FLOW analysis of 55 stations and continuous waveforms from January 2021 to July 2022 identified 53,838 events around China’s Baihetan Reservoir, about four times the manual catalog, with reported relative location errors of tens of metres and recall of about 99% of manual events. The authors infer that post-impoundment seismicity is concentrated along regional structures and fractures, differs across the Jinsha River, and includes migration away from the inundation zone and with depth. They report depth-dependent b-value and water-depth/event-rate patterns consistent with different shallow and deep controls.

15 Jan 2025BESIII Collaboration

Graph neural network enables first observation of Λc⁺ → ne⁺νe decay

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Using 4.5 fb⁻¹ of BESIII electron–positron collision data, the collaboration reports the first observation of the Cabibbo-suppressed Λc⁺ → ne⁺νe decay at greater than 10σ significance. It measures a branching fraction of (3.57 ± 0.34(stat) ± 0.14(syst)) × 10⁻³ and extracts |Vcd| = 0.208 ± 0.011(exp) ± 0.007(LQCD) ± 0.001(lifetime) for the first time from a charmed-baryon decay.

Technology: Graph neural network (GNN) trained on BESIII electromagnetic-calorimeter shower patterns

Editorial relevance 60 physics Published articlePreprint Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The GNN separated neutron signal showers from the dominant Λc⁺ → Λe⁺νe background, whose neutron-plus-π⁰ calorimeter patterns are difficult to distinguish with conventional selection or boosted trees. The authors trained and calibrated it using large J/ψ control samples, then fitted its output distributions to extract the signal yield.
Test and result
Using 4.5 fb⁻¹ of BESIII electron–positron collision data, the collaboration reports the first observation of the Cabibbo-suppressed Λc⁺ → ne⁺νe decay at greater than 10σ significance.
Limitation / still unknown
The observation is an experimental result from the BESIII data sample; the branching-fraction and |Vcd| uncertainties include statistical, systematic, lattice-QCD and lifetime components as reported. The paper studies data-versus-simulation domain shift and network uncertainty; residual modeling differences contribute to systematic uncertainty. The |Vcd| extraction depends on lattice-QCD input.

30 Dec 2024ATLAS Collaboration; CERN Large Hadron Collider

OmniFold enables an unbinned 24-dimensional Z+jets cross-section measurement by ATLAS

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Using 139 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton-proton data, ATLAS simultaneously unfolded 24 correlated Z+jets kinematic observables with the machine-learning method OmniFold and released the particle-level result as an unbinned event dataset. The measured total fiducial cross section is 1,808 ± 42 fb; most illustrative differential bins have 3–5% total uncertainty, rising to about 15% in tails. The measurement is more precise than the compared predictions for many distributions, especially Sherpa. The event-level release also supports derived observables, including an infrared-and-collinear-safe τ₂₁ measurement in a specified fiducial region.

Technology: OmniFold iterative machine-learning unfolding

Editorial relevance 60 physics Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
OmniFold used neural-network classifiers to iteratively reweight simulated events so the detector-level simulation matched collision data, then propagated the learned correction to particle-level events. That multivariate unfolding handled 24 correlated observables simultaneously and produced event weights with uncertainty variations, enabling a high-dimensional unbinned measurement and event-level construction of additional derived observables. The paper validates closure on pseudo-data and reports its uncertainty components.
Test and result
Using 139 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton-proton data, ATLAS simultaneously unfolded 24 correlated Z+jets kinematic observables with the machine-learning method OmniFold and released the particle-level result as an unbinned event dataset.
Limitation / still unknown
This is a new high-dimensional precision measurement of known Z+jets production, not a new particle or beyond-Standard-Model discovery. The released measurement is unbinned in 24 measured variables; displayed plots may be binned for presentation. The authors report mostly 3–5% uncertainties in illustrative bins, up to about 15% in tails, with dominant unfolding-prior/hidden-variable uncertainties for some observables. Pseudo-data closure is generally good, with exceptions including a p-value of 0.038 for leading-jet transverse momentum; users are given phase-space and covariance-use guidance.

20 Nov 2024Google DeepMind; and Google Quantum AI

AlphaQubit: a neural decoder for quantum error correction

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A Transformer-based decoder trained on data from a 49-qubit Sycamore processor made 6% fewer errors than tensor-network methods and 30% fewer than correlated matching in the largest experiments. It is too slow for real-time error correction on fast superconducting processors.

Technology: AlphaQubit (Transformer)

Family AlphaQubit: profile · models page

Editorial relevance 60 physics Blog postPublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
A recurrent transformer neural network decodes surface-code syndromes to predict logical errors; it is the method under test.
Test and result
On Sycamore data, logical error per round was 2.901e-2 (distance 3) and 2.748e-2 (distance 5), versus 3.028e-2 and 2.915e-2 for a tensor-network decoder. Simulations to distance 11 (241 qubits); correlated matching was 1.25 to 1.4 times worse.
Limitation / still unknown
Throughput is slower than the 1 microsecond per round target. Training at distance 11 needs more data. Accuracy beyond distance 11 and logical computations remain future work. Larger-distance results are simulated.

20 Nov 2024Sapienza University of Rome; Pennsylvania State University

Deep learning resolves subtle seismic-wave changes across the 2016 Norcia earthquake cycle

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A seven-layer convolutional neural network classified raw seismic waveforms recorded near Italy’s 2016 Mw 6.5 Norcia earthquake as pre- or post-mainshock and separated time-to-failure intervals with over 90% test accuracy. The authors interpret the model’s performance, frequency dependence and null tests as evidence that local waveforms encode evolving attenuation and elastic properties consistent with progressive fault damage before rupture and postseismic recovery.

Technology: Seven-layer convolutional neural network trained on local seismic waveforms

Editorial relevance 40 physics Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Classified the waveform signals by their temporal relation to the mainshock; controlled tests using incorrect mainshock times, distant stations and filtered frequencies helped expose subtle local seismic-wave property changes associated with the fault cycle.
Test and result
A seven-layer convolutional neural network classified raw seismic waveforms recorded near Italy’s 2016 Mw 6.5 Norcia earthquake as pre- or post-mainshock and separated time-to-failure intervals with over 90% test accuracy.
Limitation / still unknown
Authors studied one earthquake sequence and state that generalization to other tectonic settings and prospective real-time precursor detection remain untested. Related changes in elastic properties were already reported by field studies; the paper's new contribution is applying deep learning to infer them from this local waveform sequence.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Interpreting a microfluidic droplet experiment movie with a Fourier neural operator and sparse regression yielded a KdV–Burgers-type continuum model containing a dispersive term absent from the prior Burgers description. 6 Jun 2024Michigan State University; Stanford University; University of California, Berkeley

PhaseNet-TF expands Tonga earthquake catalog and resolves the intermediate-depth double seismic zone

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A semi-supervised PhaseNet-TF workflow detected and relocated 13,616 events from a one-year Tonga OBS and land-station record, about 11 times the manually picked reference catalog. The denser catalog delineates the intermediate-depth double seismic zone to about 300 km and indicates that its lower plane is limited to roughly 19°–21°S, rather than continuous along the studied section. The study did not recover a deeper 350–460 km double zone; sparse events and location uncertainties limit inference at those depths.

Technology: PhaseNet-TF deep-learning phase picker with semi-supervised iterative retraining

Editorial relevance 53 physics Published articlePublished article Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution

This expanded catalog enabled the localized double-seismic-zone inference.
Test and result
A semi-supervised PhaseNet-TF workflow detected and relocated 13,616 events from a one-year Tonga OBS and land-station record, about 11 times the manually picked reference catalog.
Limitation / still unknown
The catalog is based on one year of OBS and land data. About half of events are outside the array and have larger hypocenter uncertainty from azimuthal gaps; velocity-model choice adds absolute-location uncertainty. The authors say the absent 350–460 km double zone may reflect insufficient events, not definitive absence. Stress and hydration explanations are interpretations, and some rare events above the slab may be false.

The reinforcement-learning controller was tested in experiments on the DIII-D tokamak, where it kept the tearing likelihood below a threshold, including under unfavorable conditions.
Limitation / still unknown
The abstract does not state the prediction horizon and lists no other limitations; tearing instability is hard to forecast.

14 Feb 2024Cornell University; The Ohio State University; Google Research; Harvard University; Ewha Womans University

Interpretable machine learning finds a perpendicular-spin signature consistent with a spinon Fermi surface

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A correlator convolutional neural network trained on projective snapshots from numerical simulations of the Kitaev–Heisenberg model identified a characteristic correlation pattern in the intermediate gapless phase when analyzing spin snapshots perpendicular to the applied field. The authors interpret the learned pattern as Friedel oscillations of gapless spinons forming a Fermi surface, supporting earlier theoretical proposals for this phase and suggesting measurable correlations for future experiments.

9 Feb 2024Cornell University; Stanford University; Argonne National Laboratory; University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign

Machine-learning X-ray analysis provides bulk evidence for a Bragg-glass phase in PdₓErTe₃

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Researchers combined extensive X-ray diffraction with X-ray Temperature Clustering (X-TEC) to identify Bragg-glass signatures in the charge-density-wave material PdₓErTe₃. Their analysis extracted diffraction features across about 20,000 Brillouin zones and found a diverging correlation length and a phase diagram consistent with a Bragg-glass transition. The result provides bulk diffraction evidence for a phase that had been difficult to establish experimentally.

Test and result
Researchers combined extensive X-ray diffraction with X-ray Temperature Clustering (X-TEC) to identify Bragg-glass signatures in the charge-density-wave material PdₓErTe₃.
Limitation / still unknown
The authors frame the result as evidence for the Bragg-glass phase based on characteristic diffraction behavior, rather than direct imaging of all underlying defects. Machine learning enabled the scale of peak extraction; physical interpretation and measurements were done by the team.

18 Jan 2024Chinese Academy of Sciences; Zhejiang Ocean University; collaborators

Deep learning reveals a new seismicity distribution along the Kabaw Fault in central Myanmar

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A deep-learning-based catalog pipeline processed 18 months of seismic-array data and located 1,891 shallow earthquakes, more than twice the 849 events in the prior manual catalog. The denser catalog shows that shallow Kabaw Fault seismicity disappears south of about 22.8°N while deeper events occur west of the fault, and it fills part of a previously under-sampled seismicity gap; the authors interpret this contrast as potentially reflecting along-strike variation in deformation associated with oblique Indian-plate subduction.

2 Jan 2024GFZ Helmholtz Centre for Geosciences; University of Potsdam; Stanford University

Deep clustering reveals weak tremor three days before the 2021 Geldingadalir eruption

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Unsupervised deep clustering of historical seismic recordings revealed a weak tremor beginning around midday on 16 March 2021, roughly three days before the Geldingadalir eruption in Iceland. Harmonic–percussive signal separation on the same recordings supported the timing. The analysis covered one eruption and relied on one seismic station. Prospective eruption forecasting remains untested. The finding was reported in a preprint on 5 May 2023 and published in Communications Earth & Environment on 2 January 2024.

Test and result

Limitation / still unknown
Recurrence across eruptions, discrimination from non-eruptive unrest, and prospective alert performance remain unestablished.
